# Community Management Readme

## Practical entries

Use this framework when people need useful participation, relationships or joint contributions: help one another, take part in a valued activity, keep shared work feasible, or make their experience useful elsewhere. A community organiser, participant, practitioner, sponsor or assisting agent can enter at the difficulty already present. When the proposed community is meant to support a wider cultural purpose, CB.1 also helps decide whether that purpose needs a community contribution at all.

These are selected examples, not a catalogue or a coverage boundary. Bring the actual question to the [Table of Contents](#table-of-contents) when none fits. Open a pattern's Problem frame, then its Solution and worked case. You can ask an assisting agent: “Explain this and give me your comments in ordinary language, without framework jargon.”

### OFFER — Choose what would help

- **Situation:** You have been asked to start or grow a community, but people may need an existing resource, independent practice, expert help or company with one another.
- **Question:** Which contribution is worth making for these people under the available conditions?
- **First useful result or blocker:** A credible offer and a bounded action to obtain a response, or the particular missing contribution that prevents an honest offer.
- **Start with:** [CB.1](#cb1---choose-a-useful-cultural-or-community-contribution). Its comparison includes ways to develop carriers of a practice without requiring mutual communication.
- **Stop or return:** Keep an adequate existing arrangement. When the offer is sound and the remaining task is a first exchange, move to CB.2; when a professional method or permission is missing, obtain it from its actual supplier.

### EXCHANGE — Make one request lead to useful help

- **Situation:** People have joined a group, but a shareable question still has no relevant, willing contributor or useful answer.
- **Question:** How can this particular exchange be completed?
- **First useful result or blocker:** The recipient obtains a usable next step, connection or answer and returns what it helped them do, or the organiser identifies the unavailable expertise, material, access or time.
- **Start with:** [CB.2](#cb2---arrange-the-first-useful-exchanges). Use CB.5 when the difficulty is choosing and carrying out a richer contribution such as a case clinic or project review.
- **Stop or return:** An urgent accountable answer may require a service. Reconsider the offer through CB.1 when the central contribution repeatedly cannot be obtained.

### CONTINUE — Carry a voluntary activity through a change of organiser

- **Situation:** An organiser is leaving. People want some activity to continue, while access, funding or the right to decide remains unsettled.
- **Question:** What can continue, on whose terms, with whom actually able to perform it?
- **First useful result or blocker:** An agreed feasible continuation or ending. For a transfer, someone can take over and completes a first activity under the new arrangement; otherwise the missing mandate, capability or resource remains explicit. The useful activity, decision mandate, willing work with its means, first performance, settlement of what ends, and a return of what happened need to remain connected through the change.
- **Start with:** [CB.11](#cb11---continue-change-transfer-or-end-a-community-arrangement); use [CB.13](#cb13---establish-a-workable-mandate-for-shared-decisions) for missing mandate, [CB.7](#cb7---prepare-and-sustain-distributed-organising) for organising capability and [CB.9](#cb9---provide-for-community-work-and-agree-its-terms) for provision and terms.
- **Stop or return:** Reduce, pause or end an unsupported promise. Keep an adequate existing arrangement. An unresolved incident still needs its responsible receiver under CB.8.

## How to use the language

Use the method that answers your present difficulty. Choose the smallest contribution that can answer the situation, obtain its real inputs, perform the relevant work, and inspect what reached the intended person or action. The [Architectural Reference](#community-management-principles-framework-architectural-reference) explains how to connect methods and why the alternatives matter. The [Applications](#applications) show complete constructed uses across unlike settings.

The four Parts group related questions for finding them. They are not stages that every community must traverse. You can begin with a resource dispute, an incident, a missing contribution or a useful existing practice. Follow another pattern when you need its result. Permission refused, unavailable expertise or a burden that nobody accepts can stop the proposed action.

# Community Management Principles Framework Architectural Reference

## CB.Reference:1 - Useful participation and its wider contributions

Community Management concerns building, sustaining, changing and ending arrangements for useful participation, relationships and joint contributions. Its Methods serve organisers and participants, including people who do this work without a community-manager position. The word management grants no authority over participants: the actual permissions, accepted commitments and common mandate determine who can decide what.

A community can support a wider cultural purpose. That purpose can also be served by people or agents who obtain and use a practice independently. A population carrying a practice need not communicate, identify with a community or act as one decision-making body. C.36 and C.36.RP address that wider cultural question directly; this framework supplies the community contribution and the qualified alternatives described in its patterns.

This language serves people constructing or changing such contributions in professional networks, employee and customer communities, voluntary groups, communities of practice and other shared activities. The immediate difficulty may be an unclear offer, an unanswered request, inaccessible participation, exhausted organisers, a disputed rule, a missing shared resource, a practice that nobody can reuse, or a collective view that cannot reach a decision. People may also want more of an already useful activity. The framework helps turn that intent into something people can actually obtain and sustain.

Begin by distinguishing the intended change. A repairer may want a justified diagnostic continuation; a neighbour may want enjoyable company; a volunteer may need a manageable contribution; a sponsor may want more capable use of a product. Learning, relationships, cultural continuation and commercial outcomes can coexist, but one does not stand for the others. Their differences can change the offer, the appropriate method and who should bear its cost.

An employee community and the organization employing its participants are different ways of considering their relations. Assignment authority over a job does not automatically settle a community's participation terms; a community mandate does not authorize changes to the employer's production commitments. A provider can contribute to several communities without controlling them. Distinguish these relations when a proposed contribution relies on them.

Use an existing satisfactory arrangement without re-engineering it. When one person needs a dependable professional answer, obtain the service. When an adequate explanation or learning provision lets people perform independently, that can be the whole cultural contribution. When there is no usable professional method, develop or obtain that method before promising its benefits. Community activity can help that work, but does not supply the missing domain knowledge by itself.

## CB.Reference:2 - What makes the choice difficult

People need a useful result without having unlimited time to help obtain it. Organising requires preparation, matching, access, follow-up and judgement, much of which remains invisible in an attendance count. A lively event can depend on unrepeatable founder work; a quiet resource can support valuable independent use. Count and visibility therefore answer only some of the practical questions.

Participation must fit different circumstances. Newcomers may need a small explained first action, while experienced members may need freedom to contribute occasionally. Recognition can acknowledge useful work, yet an unwanted public award can make participation harder. A sponsor may supply space or money while seeking control that defeats the participant's purpose. Volunteers can accept a bounded contribution without accepting a continuing production obligation.

Common work creates decisions about access, upkeep, rules and representation. The person who owns a room, the person who can teach a practice and the person authorised to speak for participants may be different people. Obtain permission from its holder, qualified help from a capable practitioner, and a mandate from those entitled to grant it. Keep an existing decision arrangement when it meets the shared need and provides appropriate consultation.

Evidence also has a cost. A returned account of one application may reveal a repair worth making. It does not estimate the prevalence of the problem or establish a general causal effect. Wider causal, learning or professional claims need the evidence appropriate to those claims. Choose that additional work when the decision depends on it.

## CB.Reference:3 - Connect the methods through the contribution needed next

Start from the working question and use any relevant results already obtained elsewhere. The following connections explain common choices; they do not prescribe a universal workflow.

[CB.1](#cb1---choose-a-useful-cultural-or-community-contribution) compares possible contributions and makes a credible offer. When mutual help is selected, [CB.2](#cb2---arrange-the-first-useful-exchanges) arranges a finishable exchange and obtains the recipient's account of its use. If the central contribution repeatedly cannot be obtained, reconsider the offer before recruiting more people. If the selected route is independent learning or a qualified service, obtain those contributions directly.

[CB.3](#cb3---make-entry-and-changes-of-participation-workable) makes a next participation action understandable and feasible, including a reduction, return or departure. [CB.4](#cb4---design-a-space-and-shared-activity-for-its-purpose) connects a space and shared activity to its intended result. [CB.5](#cb5---obtain-and-return-useful-contributions-from-participants) develops the actual contribution: a case clinic, a demonstration, a project review, joint work or another fitting form. A discussion setting supplies opportunities for interaction; prepared material, capable participants and the recipient's next use make that interaction useful.

[CB.6](#cb6---recognise-a-contribution-and-choose-a-meaningful-response) connects recognition to the contribution and the person's circumstances. [CB.7](#cb7---prepare-and-sustain-distributed-organising) obtains capable, supported organising work, including temporary participant groups where shared attention is needed, and an absence arrangement where continuity matters. [CB.8](#cb8---make-participation-rules-usable-and-resolve-incidents) makes expectations usable and carries incidents through judgement, response and correction. These methods address different problems: praise does not pay a promised fee, a role title does not prepare an organiser, and a welcoming tone does not settle a contested incident.

[CB.9](#cb9---provide-for-community-work-and-agree-its-terms) connects a promise to qualified time, access, funds and agreed terms. [CB.10](#cb10---understand-the-value-of-participation-and-cultural-work) examines particular uses and outcomes, including burdens, failures and unrealised potential, and returns a useful change. [CB.11](#cb11---continue-change-transfer-or-end-a-community-arrangement) uses those results to compare continuation, reduction, transfer, separation, pause and ending. An agreed transfer becomes effective when the receiver can perform the work and the former arrangement's obligations are settled.

[CB.12](#cb12---support-a-useful-repeated-action-of-participation) supports a wanted repeated action through a fitting occasion, feasible action and useful return. [CB.13](#cb13---establish-a-workable-mandate-for-shared-decisions) establishes a mandate where common decisions need one. Repetition is not the purpose of every participation arrangement; shared decision rights are not needed for every independent cultural use.

[CB.14](#cb14---develop-and-maintain-a-shared-repertoire-of-practice) develops a repertoire that practitioners can use, question and maintain. It can select simple documentation, reciprocal comparison or a properly provisioned development project. [CB.15](#cb15---connect-practices-through-a-useful-joint-contribution) connects practices through a useful joint contribution, keeping differences in meaning and working conditions visible. [CB.16](#cb16---form-and-carry-a-collective-voice-into-a-decision) carries a collective contribution into another party's decision with a position, mandate and returned answer. A useful idea can move between people without a joint method-development programme; an individual expert can speak without claiming to represent everyone.

When combining these contributions, establish their joint feasibility. An expert may be unable to advise on a case and welcome newcomers during the same hour; one room may not support two activities at the required time. Obtain another contribution, change timing or reduce the promise. An appeal concerning that expert's own response needs another reviewer; rescheduling alone cannot supply independence. Each local method may be sensible while the combination is impossible.

## CB.Reference:4 - Two ways to carry a practice, and why the distinction matters

An **arrangement based on mutual participation** makes contributions available through people working or spending time with one another. It may be a case exchange, music group, shared workshop or professional network. The arrangement must obtain suitable participants, material, interaction, support and a return to use. Its benefit can include valued relationships or an enjoyable experience; it need not be justified as a course or commercial acquisition mechanism.

An **arrangement for independent use** makes a practice obtainable and usable in people's separate settings. It may rely on a description, demonstration, exercise, tool and correction route. Cultural continuation then depends on actual acquisition, performance, selection and retention of the practice. A forum is optional. The [independent-use application](#cbapplication3---application-3---extend-a-practice-without-a-discussion-group) shows how a missing resource changes this route even when the learner understands the method.

These alternatives can be combined within one person's changing participation. Someone can learn independently, ask one question, contribute an example and then return to private work. CB.3 supports that change when participation itself needs help. [FPF C.36](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#c36---cultural-evolution-and-cultural-evolution-engineering) supplies the wider distinctions among cultural transmission, performance, retention and renewal. [FPF C.36.RP](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#c36rp---sustain-and-renew-shared-ways-of-working) develops how to make a way of working obtainable, usable and renewable across carriers. CB.1 compares the proposed community contribution with serious alternatives. Its independent-learning branch assumes a sufficiently known professional practice and obtainable learning provision; it does not supply every kind of cultural construction.

For example, a capable AI may already perform a selected contribution, while a person needs an explanation, a permitted tool or a professional service rather than learning that performance themselves. Obtain the missing contribution through its actual Method and provider. Use HCD when a human capability is the intended result. Use CB when participation or a joint contribution still needs arranging. C.36.RP keeps the wider acquisition, provision and renewal question available when no such participation is needed.

The language therefore starts from the receiving situation rather than a platform, a membership target or one inherited community model. It retains the useful organising repertoire found in practitioner sources while asking what each move contributes. Communities of practice supply powerful forms for joint learning and stewardship, yet learning together does not define every community or every cultural intervention. Business community methods supply purpose, provision and early organising constructions, while participants' own purposes and voluntary terms remain explicit.

This organization accepts some additional inquiry before choosing a format. It avoids committing people to recurring interaction when an adequate reference or individual service would suffice, and avoids publishing a resource as if it could replace the interpretation or relationship people actually need. When a new case defeats those alternatives, return to the particular contribution and its conditions, not to a universal claim that communities should grow or become more active.

## CB.Reference:5 - The work being performed, and the support it needs

A method in this book often describes a constituent of larger work. In the repairer application, asking for a discriminating observation is part of a peer exchange; that exchange is part of an attempt to obtain a diagnostic continuation; and the repairer's diagnostic work serves an actual customer's maintenance need. The customer commitment changes the peer exchange while it is happening. If the answer must be accountable and available within an hour, an optional evening group cannot carry that demand.

Another condition can change the constituent action without changing its conversational form. When the needed equipment record is confidential, the case owner first obtains permission or prepares a sufficient shareable case. A facilitator can support clarification but cannot replace the practitioner competent to interpret the observation. If that intermediate competence is absent, an orderly conversation still cannot produce the promised technical result.

The same relation applies to voluntary activity. Welcoming a newcomer may be part of running a garden session. Safe access to the shared tools, suitable supervision and the session host's available attention constrain the welcome. If the host must simultaneously resolve an urgent access incident, a prepared small task with another capable, willing helper may keep entry feasible; otherwise the session's promise must shrink.

Community Management provides only part of this work. The relevant practice supplies its professional method and correctness criteria. Human Capability Development (HCD) supplies development of a needed human capability and the conditions for practice and feedback. Organization Change Engineering (OCE) and Operations Management (OPS) help establish work arrangements and capacity when those contributions are missing; Management Accounting (MA) helps compare costs created by the chosen promise. Core's Method-development family and Research Method Practice (RMP) supply method development or research when those questions are open. The [supplier returns](#cbreference8---actual-supplier-returns) identify where those contributions become useful.

A community can support a culture of continuing development. Participation, learning a Method and creating a useful repertoire are possible contributions to that purpose; none by itself establishes development of the person, organization or other intended subject. [FPF C.40.OD](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#c40od---construct-and-continue-open-ended-development) with [FPF C.40.NP](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#c40np---continue-from-a-result-to-new-attainable-problems) supplies the connected development construction, including formation of a worthwhile question, an attainable changed possibility for that subject and continuation after success. Use CB for the participation and joint contributions that construction actually needs. The community may remain useful for ordinary work when no development undertaking is needed.

Replacing a contribution requires examining what it did in the whole. A written answer may replace an explanation of a known procedure while leaving a new case's diagnosis unresolved. Paying a coordinator may supply booking and material preparation while leaving expert capacity unchanged. Keep these differences visible when comparing alternatives and receiving the changed work.

## CB.Reference:6 - Recognise the problem, then establish the result

Recognition guides entry. An unanswered case suggests CB.2 or CB.5; recurring organiser overload suggests CB.7 or CB.9; a contested right to decide suggests CB.13. These observations do not establish that the selected arrangement works. Establish the result at the scope on which someone will rely.

For one combined use, ask:

- Who should obtain what, and do those people recognise the proposed benefit and participation terms?
- Which actual contributions, permissions and resources make that result possible, including preparation and follow-up?
- Can those contributions coexist at the required occasion, and who can change the promise when they cannot?
- What reached the next person or action? What remains an offer, a possible later benefit, a failed use or an unresolved question?
- Where do people obtain correction, change their involvement or decline an additional obligation?
- Which observed condition should reopen the offer, method, provision or continuation decision?

These questions inherit the detailed checks of the selected patterns. A new confidential case needs its own permission judgement; it does not require redesigning an otherwise adequate recognition practice. A changed room cost may reopen provision while leaving the diagnostic method intact.

The [worked applications](#applications) show the language's gain and cost. Organisers obtain more specific promises and can correct a failed condition; participants obtain clearer choices and usable contributions. The price is real preparation, follow-up and sometimes a smaller offer. The constructed cases explain decisions and operations. Evidence about the effects of using this pattern language would require field inquiry.

Whose account is easiest to hear can bias the result. Include a consequential missing perspective when a claim depends on it: a person who declined, a quiet user, a newcomer, an unpaid organiser or someone affected by a collective statement. Do not turn that inquiry into a demand for universal visibility. Ask for proportionate, permitted information and preserve useful disagreement.

## CB.Reference:7 - What this publication preserves and where to return

This publication selects sixteen recurring problems of community work and its cultural contributions and develops methods for answering them. The complete bodies preserve their recognition conditions, construction, alternatives, worked cases, costs and return conditions. The ToC compresses each to a search row; the Readme compresses selected uses to entry guidance; the Architectural Reference explains connections. None of these compressed forms replaces the body needed to perform or adapt a method.

The source basis combines practitioner community-building accounts, the communities-of-practice repertoire, conceptual distinctions about culture and communities, bounded research contributions, and current FPF and domain-method suppliers. The [source-use account](#cbsources1---source-use-and-limits) states what was adopted, adapted or bounded and which changes would reopen it. Each body's SoTA-Echoing section gives the closer comparison and source return.

The framework deliberately leaves the full history and vocabulary of each source, platform-specific implementation, the complete professional methods used by a community, and the full learning, research or commercial product outside this edition. It also coarsens variation across local cultures and institutions. Return to the original source for a claim about its evidence or broader theory; to the domain supplier for work requiring its full method; and to the people and conditions of use before applying a general example as a local arrangement.

Unresolved use, repeated misunderstanding, changed provision or new source evidence should reopen the smallest affected method or relation. Inspect a proposed repair through the actual work and recipient result, including a contrasting condition that could defeat it. Wider causal or general effectiveness claims require further inquiry. The [renewal route](#cbreference9---renew-the-affected-contribution) keeps that work distinct from changing a link, publishing an edition or continuing the activity itself.

## CB.Reference:8 - Actual supplier returns

Use the external contribution when the receiving question requires it. The cited frameworks retain their own content and edition boundaries; these returns do not include them inside Community Management.

| Receiving difficulty | Contribution to obtain and its direct supplier |
| --- | --- |
| The community is intended to support continuing development rather than only repeated participation | [FPF C.40.OCN](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#c40ocn---construct-a-bounded-opportunity), [FPF C.40.OD](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#c40od---construct-and-continue-open-ended-development) and [STR.16](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/STRATEGY-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#str16---construct-a-feasible-portfolio-with-shared-or-branch-specific-support) supply the development opportunity, connected undertaking and joint-portfolio Methods. CB supplies the participation, contributions and shared repertoire needed by that use; development of the community, its provider and a participant remain different questions. |
| A proposed cultural intervention confuses transmission, performance, retention or renewal | [FPF C.36](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#c36---cultural-evolution-and-cultural-evolution-engineering) supplies the cultural distinctions. [FPF C.36.RP](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#c36rp---sustain-and-renew-shared-ways-of-working) develops availability, acquisition, use and renewal of a shared way, including across independent carriers. CB.1 compares a community contribution with alternatives; CB.11/14 apply their own continuation and repertoire constructions. |
| The proposed community benefit lacks a serious alternative or supporting conditions | [STR.5](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/STRATEGY-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#str5---generate-strategic-directions) develops comparison of useful contributions and provision. CB.1 applies that construction to the cultural or community offer. |
| Work, help, access or authority is inferred from a role name | [OCE.2](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/ORGANIZATION-CHANGE-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#oce2---recover-current-organization-work-and-arrangement) recovers the actual work and relations. [OCE.11 (OCE.11:4)](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/ORGANIZATION-CHANGE-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#oce114---solution) arranges coexistence with continuing service; [LDR.1:4.4](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/LEADERSHIP-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#ldr144---perform-the-smallest-sufficient-working-conversation) supplies working conversations; [OCE.14 (OCE.14:4)](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/ORGANIZATION-CHANGE-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#oce144---solution) revises an established organizational arrangement from qualified results. CB.13 obtains an initial or renewed common mandate when that is missing. |
| A person needs to acquire a practice or receive useful learning support | [HCD.5](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-CAPABILITY-DEVELOPMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#hcd5---select-human-capability-development-methods) and [HCD.6](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-CAPABILITY-DEVELOPMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#hcd6---design-representative-practice-tasks) supply method selection, representative practice and feedback; [HCD.7](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-CAPABILITY-DEVELOPMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#hcd7---arrange-providers-access-tools-and-ai-support-for-human-capability-development) and [HCD.8](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-CAPABILITY-DEVELOPMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#hcd8---obtain-or-build-missing-human-capability-development-support-with-bounded-recursion) help obtain providers, access and missing learning support. [HCD.2](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-CAPABILITY-DEVELOPMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#hcd2---compose-and-compare-human-capability-development-programmes-for-later-work) compares programmes for a particular person. A complete service may also need SYSE contributions. |
| A teaching publication in Guide or Slidement form is being produced | Learning Product LPF is a separate Russian-language local practice framework for producing those formats. This publication does not supply its text. Obtain the text and relevant production practice separately; if they are unavailable, that teaching-publication contribution remains unprovided. HCD's learning-method and support contributions remain available for other parts of the arrangement. |
| A practice is known but poorly expressed, or a new method claim must be developed | [Core Method-development patterns (FPF.Reference:16.7)](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#fpfreference167---develop-and-use-methods-with-distinct-claims-and-results) supply recovery, description, development, trial, adaptation and maintenance. [RMP.1 (RMP.1:4)](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/RESEARCH-METHOD-PRACTICE-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#rmp14---solution) distinguishes sufficient existing knowledge from a research need. CB.14 organises the practitioners' contribution and its use; it does not replace those methods. |
| A community promise exceeds capable time, access or funding | [OPS.5 (OPS.5:4)](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/OPERATIONS-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#ops54---solution) supplies capacity reasoning; OPS.14 handles timing of an actual payment commitment; [MA.1](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/MANAGEMENT-ACCOUNTING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#ma1---build-the-resource-consumption-and-cost-model) and [MA.2](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/MANAGEMENT-ACCOUNTING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#ma2---explain-the-cost-and-use-of-capacity) supply the selected cost object and relevant cost account. CB.9 obtains the actual contributions and terms. |
| A scarce shared resource requires use and upkeep rules, or a rule must change | [ECO.7](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/ECONOMIC-REASONING-AND-COORDINATION-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#eco7---coordinate-use-and-renewal-of-a-shared-resource) and [ECO.9](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/ECONOMIC-REASONING-AND-COORDINATION-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#eco9---decide-whether-and-how-to-change-economic-rules-through-their-working-practices) provide methods for arranging shared use and changing resource rules. CB.8/13 connects them to participation, mandate and correction where those are needed. |
| A result is used as a causal explanation | [FPF C.28](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#c28---causaluse-cal-causal-use-questions-identification-and-realizability) distinguishes the causal-use question and necessary support; RMP supplies a fitting inquiry when required. CB.10's account of particular value does not replace that work. |
| A complete service, tool or provider arrangement is missing | [Systems Engineering (SYSE)](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/SYSTEMS-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) provides methods for defining the whole system and choosing how to obtain it. CB.1/9 can identify and arrange the community contribution within that larger provision. |

For a reproducible use or comparison, identify the supplying edition or revision actually read. These links provide discovery; the table identifies the relied-on contribution and receiving use. A supplier must still provide the needed result under the conditions of the present work.

A later edition of a supplier reopens only the CB claims and uses that rely on its changed contribution. For example, a change to the qualification of a method trial affects the CB.14 trial claim; it does not by itself reopen the garden's session-time decision. Removing the adequate professional method from the independent-use application would invalidate its chosen learning route: obtain or develop that method before continuing the same promise.

A new or unknown professional method, a complete service and a commercial strategy retain their own development questions. Use the available contributions identified above, and obtain any further result needed by the particular community's promise before relying on it.

## CB.Reference:9 - Renew the affected contribution

Begin with the changed fact and the use it can defeat. A failed invitation may concern access or wording; repeated inability to obtain qualified help may defeat the offer itself. A disputed incident may require correction of one response, a changed rule or a renewed mandate. New evidence about a professional method can invalidate a repertoire entry while leaving the social connection useful.

Return to the smallest affected source, method or arrangement. Check the proposed change through the receiving work and a consequential contrasting condition. Reuse unchanged contributions whose conditions still hold. Obtain the additional evidence required for any stronger causal, professional or population claim. Changes to representation, common commitments or the right to use resources need the relevant people's decisions as well as a revised description.

For a reader relying on this pattern language, repeated failure to obtain its promised contribution puts the affected explanation, example or practical entry in question. A change in the promised field or useful pattern division changes the framework's organization. A changed source or external method first affects its particular adopted contribution and dependent CB uses. A broken link prevents the source return; a repaired link establishes access to text, not the text's adequacy.

Continuity of the language includes a usable return when a published pattern's title, order or interpretation changes. CB.1–16 retain their local addresses within this framework; Part and section positions are publication navigation. A preserved old address keeps the continuing practical answer reachable. A different answer requires an explicit replacement or migration explanation, so that a previous citation does not silently acquire a new meaning.

For source qualifications, citation and edition information, see the [Sources and Edition Notes](#community-management-principles-framework-sources-and-edition-notes).

## CB.Reference:End

# Part A - Choose the contribution and make entry possible

## CB.1 - Choose a Useful Cultural or Community Contribution

> **Type:** Community-building method pattern
> **Status:** Stable

### CB.1:1 - Problem frame

Use this pattern when you want to help a practice spread, enable people to help one another, or create a place where a particular group can participate meaningfully, and the useful contribution is still unclear. A professional association proposes a forum; a neighbourhood organiser proposes a shared activity; a sponsor offers a platform. Before building the arrangement, determine whose situation it should improve and why this arrangement could do so.

Community Management concerns useful participation, relationships and joint contributions. This pattern begins early enough to discover that a proposed community is unnecessary. Its immediate subject is a proposed change in people's practice, participation or relationships and the contribution that could support that change. Cultural continuation is the wider question supplied by C.36 and C.36.RP. People can adopt a practice without communicating with one another; where mutual participation matters, its contribution needs an explanation of its own.

The first useful result is a bounded, understandable proposal: who could benefit, what would become possible, what participants and organisers would contribute, what conditions must hold, and which first action could test the proposal. A justified choice to use an existing service, improve access to a useful resource, or leave an adequate arrangement alone is also a result.

The intended user knows the activity in question or can obtain a qualified account from someone who does. This pattern helps organise a contribution; the activity's own professional methods supply correctness and specialist permissions.

**Ordinary non-use.** If an existing proposal already fits the people, purpose and conditions and the remaining difficulty is obtaining a first exchange, use CB.2. If one person needs help choosing a learning programme, use HCD.2. Return here when the proposed community activity itself may be the wrong response.

### CB.1:2 - Problem

An attractive community idea can hide several different problems. People may lack access to a reliable answer, opportunities to practise, trusted relationships, permission to use data, or a way to improve the practice itself. A discussion space addresses some of these conditions and leaves others intact. Its sponsor, active contributors and quiet participants may also want different results.

How can an organiser construct and compare useful contributions without treating a platform, shared label or sponsor objective as the answer?

### CB.1:3 - Forces

| Force | Decision it changes |
| --- | --- |
| A recognisable common concern | A sufficiently specific concern helps people decide whether to participate; a narrow description can also exclude people whose experience is needed. |
| Participants' benefit and organisers' purpose | A sponsor may sustain the arrangement, while its preferred behaviour may provide little value to participants. |
| Relationship and independent use | Some results need continuing mutual help; others can be obtained by people using an explanation or service independently. |
| Early uncertainty and credible commitment | A small practical probe can clarify the proposal, but participants still need truthful expectations about help, effort and control. |
| Diversity of situations and a feasible first action | One shared offer can contain several useful forms of participation; trying to serve every situation at once can prevent any exchange from working. |

### CB.1:4 - Solution

Start with one receiving situation, develop a small set of different ways to improve it, and make the chosen contribution concrete enough for affected people to question or try. Keep the cultural result, the mutual-participation result and the sponsor's result distinguishable.

**Working reminder:** find whose situation should change; compare ways to help; establish the needed contributions; make one honest offer; revise it from the response.

#### CB.1:4.1 - Recover the situation from people's work and experience

Ask a person who might use the arrangement to describe a recent relevant occasion. What were they trying to do or experience? What happened? Where did they turn, what did they receive, and what remained difficult? For an opportunity without a recent occurrence, retain the proposed situation as a hypothesis and say what makes it worth exploring.

Follow the detail that can change the proposal. A repairer saying “I need a community” may need help interpreting an unusual observation. Another may already know the diagnostic method but lack access to equipment data. A third may want colleagues with whom uncertainty can be discussed without embarrassment. These situations support different contributions even when all three people use the same occupational label.

Ask about existing help, including arrangements the organiser does not provide. A professional contact, a paid adviser, a manual, a local group or a private conversation can already answer the question. Establish what is missing from those alternatives before proposing another maintained activity.

Look beyond the founder's familiar contacts when their experience could misrepresent the intended use. Seek a consequential contrasting account: someone using another tool, working at another time, declining the original invitation, or facing a different access condition. Explain why their situation matters. This is purposeful inquiry, not a claim that a few conversations represent a whole population.

Use OCE.2 when the proposal depends on actual organisational work, informal help, access or authority that an organisation chart does not establish. Its result can show, for example, that the person everyone consults has no time allocated for that help. Preserve what the evidence establishes, what someone reports and what remains a proposal.

#### CB.1:4.2 - Separate the changes being sought

Describe each relevant change in ordinary language. Examples include being able to perform a known diagnostic procedure, obtaining a qualified second view on an unusual case, finding company for an activity, having one's experience heard, or developing a better practice with others.

For each proposed result, ask who values it and what would let that person recognise it. A participant can value a conversation or a sense of welcome without producing a document. In that case, ask about the experience under conditions in which an honest answer is possible. If the promise is improved professional work, obtain evidence about that work as well.

Keep three distinctions when they change action:

- **Access and capability:** possession of instructions or membership of a group does not establish that someone can use the practice.
- **Participation and relationships:** attending one event can be useful without creating a continuing relationship.
- **Participant and sponsor results:** product adoption or reduced support cost may matter to a sponsor, while the participant needs competent help or an opportunity to contribute.

These results can support one another. They need not be maximised together. Record a consequential disagreement rather than converting the sponsor's preferred measure into everybody's purpose.

#### CB.1:4.3 - Construct alternatives by how they would help

Generate alternatives that change the way the result is obtained. STR.5 supplies the fuller method for developing contrasting accounts of a worthwhile contribution and how it can be sustained. For a small community decision, compare the serious alternatives already exposed by the receiving situation.

| Receiving difficulty | A plausible arrangement | What must be available |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The answer is known and repeatedly needed | A usable explanation or maintained knowledge resource | Correct content, findability, an understandable scope and a way to notice outdated advice |
| A particular person needs a qualified answer under a dependable service commitment | A professional consultation or existing support service | Competence, a feasible response arrangement, access and an accountable provider |
| People need to perform a known practice independently | Suitable explanation, practice, feedback and access | A usable domain method, representative tasks, appropriate help and the conditions for later use |
| Different people hold useful experience and want to exchange it | A bounded mutual-help arrangement | Relevant requests, willing capable contributors, permissible material and enough coordination |
| People want continuing contact or shared activity | An accessible meeting or recurring shared practice | A purpose participants recognise, a workable form, welcome and an acceptable participation burden |

An existing adequate arrangement remains a serious alternative. Combine arrangements when each supplies a necessary contribution: a reference resource can answer routine questions while a small peer group handles unusual cases. Explain where the person changes route, so that “community support” does not become an unlimited promise.

Compare at the scale of the actual first use. Include preparation, response, correction, coordination and the participant's effort, not only platform cost. Do not make a well-developed favourite defeat an alternative described only by its name. Unknown willingness or capacity remains unknown until an appropriate response establishes it.

#### CB.1:4.4 - Build the contribution for the selected branch

For **mutual participation**, name the kinds of people whose contributions can help one another and the activity through which that help becomes possible. Describe the minimum relevant experience or access, the material people can bring, and what someone receiving a contribution will do with it. Membership criteria should follow the work and conditions of participation. A brand preference or friendship with the founder is insufficient when the promise concerns a wider professional group.

For **independent carriers of a sufficiently known practice who need to learn it**, identify the population and later activity to be changed. C.36 distinguishes transmission, performance, selection and renewal; C.36.RP supplies the broader construction when access, a capable provider, tools or method development may answer the need. The learning branch below connects contributions for its particular result:

1. Make the useful activity and participation demand understandable to potential learners. Show a relevant use and the available means of learning it.
2. Establish that the domain method and its correctness criteria are available. If experts cannot supply a usable method, return that question to Method Engineering, and to Research Method Practice when unresolved evidence requires inquiry.
3. Use HCD.5 to select learning activities for the relevant starting conditions and HCD.6 to construct representative practice and changed-condition attempts. Obtain missing material, help or access through HCD.7/8; a teaching publication can use Learning Product LPF.
4. Observe whether people can carry out the target activity under the support conditions of their later work. Keep course participation, helped performance and independent performance distinct.
5. Arrange how revised instructions, questions or observed failures can reach the people concerned. They may receive these from a provider without joining a discussion group.

This limited composition works when the professional practice is sufficiently known and the learning provision can actually be obtained. It remains a useful alternative to forming a community. If the needed result is available through direct performance, access or an adequate tool, use that contribution without imposing a learning programme. If the question is the design of a whole new service, return the contribution and delivery requirements to Systems Engineering. For an unavailable or no longer usable shared way, C.36.RP develops acquisition, reconstruction, provision and renewal. The organiser retains the question of whether the intended population can obtain and use the resulting provision; the direct supplier retains its substantive Method.

#### CB.1:4.5 - Make an offer that people can assess

Express the proposal in language potential participants use. Include the situation it addresses, the benefit being proposed, the first activity, the contribution requested from them, and what the organiser or provider will supply. State the boundaries that could change their decision: time, cost, confidentiality, commercial interest, limits of help and a feasible way to decline or leave.

A conversation or short invitation can carry these contents. Make a distinction between a service that promises a response and a voluntary opportunity to ask capable peers.

Ask recipients to explain what they expect to do and receive. Then invite an appropriately small real action: offer a permitted case, choose an available learning attempt, attend a first shared activity, or explain why the proposal does not fit. Agreement with an appealing idea is weaker evidence than a response to its actual demands. A refusal can reveal an unsuitable condition; it does not by itself show that the underlying need is absent.

Where people depend on an employer or organiser, examine what declining would actually cost them. A nominally optional activity with adverse consequences for refusal needs a different participation account.

#### CB.1:4.6 - Choose the next action and a return condition

Continue when the contribution is sufficiently worthwhile and feasible for the bounded action being proposed. For mutual help, pass CB.2 the receiving situation, first useful result, willing participants or recruitment question, material and access limits, and available organising effort. For independent learning, pass the target work and provision conditions to the selected HCD use.

Stop or change the proposal when an existing alternative answers the situation better, a necessary contribution cannot be obtained, or the apparent common purpose hides a material unresolved conflict. An unanswered question can itself be the next useful result: who can provide the required access, whether the intended recipient values the proposed result, or whether any capable person wishes to help.

Reopen the smallest affected choice when later responses change the population, desired result, available competence, burden or terms of participation. One failed invitation does not require rethinking the whole cultural purpose; repeated inability to obtain the central contribution may.

### CB.1:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### CB.1:5.1 - Independent repairers compare four ways to obtain help

This constructed case concerns twenty independent repairers who sometimes encounter unfamiliar equipment faults. A supplier offers a branded forum. Some repairers service competing products, and the supplier would prefer discussions that favour its own equipment.

The organiser first examines three stipulated accounts. One repairer spends time locating an already documented procedure. Another needs a second interpretation of an unusual observation. A third understands the procedure but lacks permitted access to the needed data. These accounts are enough to reject one undifferentiated promise of “faster repairs”; they do not estimate how frequent each difficulty is.

The organiser develops four alternatives. A maintained index to approved procedures could help the first person. A paid specialist could provide an accountable response to the second. A voluntary case exchange could also help that person when timing is flexible and suitable peers choose to participate. Neither discussion nor training supplies the third person's missing access.

The first proposal therefore combines a reference route with a small, brand-neutral case exchange. Its initial case must be shareable and within the participants' competence. The organiser explains the sponsor's involvement, permits relevant discussion of other brands and obtains agreement on what material can be retained. If the sponsor requires promotional control inconsistent with that promise, the organiser changes the sponsorship arrangement or narrows the offer honestly.

The offer is: bring one permitted diagnostic question; a small group will try to identify a justified next step during an agreed window; the case owner will report whether that step was usable. It promises a bounded attempt at peer help. It does not promise repair completion or a guaranteed response to every future case.

CB.2 receives that proposal. An urgent customer case requiring a dependable specialist response uses the service alternative instead.

#### CB.1:5.2 - More carriers without a discussion group

The same association wants more repairers to recognise one well-understood fault condition. In this constructed variant, the diagnostic method, correctness criteria and authorised training examples already exist.

The organiser makes the use and prerequisites understandable, obtains a demonstration and a practice task through the relevant HCD methods, and ensures that repairers have access to the required information in their own workshops. A changed example tests whether they can select and use the diagnostic procedure under the stated help conditions. The training provider collects fault reports and distributes corrections to the material.

This arrangement can produce the intended cultural contribution even if the repairers never talk to one another. If their remaining difficulty is interpreting genuinely new cases, the organiser can later compare peer exchange and expert consultation. If the method itself is unsettled, more enrolments would not settle it; that question returns to method development.

#### CB.1:5.3 - A voluntary activity changes the criterion of usefulness

Neighbours want a quiet place to read and talk together. The proposed result is enjoyable company and an accessible opportunity to participate. The organiser asks about timing, travel, noise and the kinds of conversation people welcome. A smaller nearby meeting may fit better than a large online group. Attendance and participants' accounts help revise the arrangement; a count of completed books would answer another question.

### CB.1:6 - Bias-Annotation

Business-sponsored community accounts can privilege growth, retention and visible contribution. The receiving-situation inquiry keeps participants' purposes, quiet use and non-commercial arrangements available. The organiser's contacts can also make one convenient audience appear to be the whole relevant population; inspect a consequential missing perspective before generalising the proposal.

### CB.1:7 - Conformance Checklist

Before relying on the proposal, check that:

- the intended people and useful change can be recognised in an actual or explicitly proposed situation;
- the alternatives differ in how they would help, and an existing adequate route was considered where relevant;
- each necessary contribution has an available supplier or a named unresolved question;
- mutual participation is required only where it contributes to the result;
- the offer exposes the effort, interests and participation conditions that could change a person's decision;
- the next action and evidence can distinguish usefulness from registration, activity or agreement with an idea.

A small proposal can satisfy these questions in ordinary prose. A stronger commitment requires stronger evidence of demand, provision and applicable domain conditions.

### CB.1:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Common wrong turn | Practical repair |
| --- | --- |
| “We have a platform, so we should build a community.” | Recover a receiving situation and compare ways to improve it before selecting the platform's role. |
| One active customer's preferences define the whole offer | Obtain a relevant contrasting account and narrow the claim to what the evidence supports. |
| A sponsor's retention target is presented as participants' purpose | Explain both purposes and the terms under which participants receive a worthwhile contribution. |
| No one accepts the first invitation, so people are judged unmotivated | Inspect relevance, requested effort, timing, trust and available alternatives; change the proposal or stop. |
| More discussion is prescribed for missing access or an unknown professional method | Return the exact missing contribution to its owner and retain any remaining community question separately. |

### CB.1:9 - Consequences

The organiser can explain why a particular community or cultural contribution is worth attempting and what would change the choice. Participants can assess a concrete offer. The work may produce a smaller arrangement, a combination with an existing service, or a useful route without mutual interaction.

This inquiry costs attention before recruitment and can expose conflicts a broad slogan concealed. Its result remains conditional where people's willingness, provision or effects have not yet been observed.

### CB.1:10 - Rationale

A useful contribution connects a person's situation to an obtainable result. Starting from that connection makes the form of association a design choice. Starting from a forum or membership label makes it difficult to notice when the required contribution is instruction, access or professional service.

Comparison also protects legitimate variation. A community for shared company and one for technical help need different criteria; neither needs to imitate a commercial support programme. Separating participant, cultural and sponsor results permits a reasoned combination without assuming that all interests coincide.

### CB.1:11 - SoTA-Echoing

The practical comparison here is between ways of obtaining the proposed contribution under comparable participant and organiser effort. It is not a claim that one community model has universal superiority.

| Source line or developed treatment | Adopted or adapted contribution | Boundary and effect on the method |
| --- | --- | --- |
| David Spinks, The Business of Belonging (2021), physical PDF pp.77–100 | Ask whom the arrangement serves, inspect recognisable identities and unmet opportunities, include missing voices, and distinguish industry participation from a product brand. | These are practitioner heuristics and reported cases. The identity cycle does not establish that every participant should become more active or that identity formation is the goal of every cultural intervention. |
| A. Levenchuk, essays on community ontology and systemicity (27–28 September 2024) | Preserve development of carriers of a practice without requiring mutual communication or a collective agent. | Used as a conceptual distinction and engineering proposal, not evidence of intervention effectiveness. |
| C.36 and STR.5 | Distinguish cultural relations and construct contrasting accounts of a worthwhile contribution with its supporting conditions. | The community-specific work connects those results to potential participants, a credible offer and an obtainable first action. |
| HCD.5/6 and OCE.2 | Match learning to the required action; reconstruct actual work, help and access rather than infer them from labels. | Reuse their concrete results. A community offer supplies neither domain correctness nor a missing permission. |

Source returns: [David Spinks, The Business of Belonging (2021)](https://www.wiley-vch.de/en/areas-interest/finance-economics-law/the-business-of-belonging-978-1-119-76612-4); [community ontology](https://ailev.livejournal.com/1735885.html); [systemicity discussion](https://ailev.livejournal.com/1736164.html). Reconsider the choice when new evidence changes the receiving situation, the useful mechanism or the feasibility of a serious alternative.

### CB.1:12 - Relations

- **Supplies CB.2:** a bounded proposal for mutual help, with the first result, participant conditions and organising capacity. A failure to obtain the central contribution returns from CB.2 to the affected choice here.
- **Uses [FPF C.36](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#c36---cultural-evolution-and-cultural-evolution-engineering):** the cultural relations and result being sought. [FPF C.36.RP](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#c36rp---sustain-and-renew-shared-ways-of-working) supplies the wider Method for making a shared way obtainable, usable and renewable; :4.4 here retains the narrower known-practice learning alternative.
- **Uses [STR.5 (STR.5:4)](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/STRATEGY-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#str54---solution):** contrasting useful contributions and their provision conditions when that choice needs further development.
- **Uses [OCE.2 (OCE.2:4)](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/ORGANIZATION-CHANGE-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#oce24---solution):** evidence about actual organisational work and relations when the proposal relies on them.
- **Uses [HCD.5:4 and HCD.6](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-CAPABILITY-DEVELOPMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#hcd54---solution):** selected learning activities and representative tasks for an independent-carrier contribution. HCD.2 serves a person's programme choice; HCD.7/8 serves missing support and access.

- **Returns missing contributions to [Method development in Core (FPF C.39)](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#c39---find-and-develop-a-way-to-obtain-a-result), [Research Method Practice (RMP)](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/RESEARCH-METHOD-PRACTICE-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) or [Systems Engineering (SYSE)](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/SYSTEMS-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md):** respectively, a usable professional method, an answer requiring inquiry, or a feasible whole provision/service construction. State the exact missing result when making the return.

### CB.1:End

## CB.2 - Arrange the First Useful Exchanges

> **Type:** Community-building method pattern
> **Status:** Stable

### CB.2:1 - Problem frame

Use this pattern when a group could benefit from mutual help or shared activity, but people have not yet found a workable way to participate together. A new professional group has interested names but unanswered questions. A local gathering attracts visitors who leave without making a useful connection. An existing group starts a new activity for which its old habits do not suffice.

The organiser's task is to arrange a bounded exchange in which someone offers a relevant contribution, another person can use or respond to it, and the participants can understand what happened. The result may be an answer, a comparison, an introduction, an agreed action or a valued conversation. Choose it from the participants' purpose.

This is the mutual-participation branch of Community Management. It begins with a credible purpose and participation conditions, such as those supplied by CB.1. It creates a first workable relationship around an activity; it does not require a formal organisation or equal contributions from every participant.

**First useful result:** a completed exchange with a known use or honest remaining gap, a feasible opportunity to continue, and an account of the help the organiser had to provide.

**Ordinary non-use.** When people can obtain the needed result independently from a resource or learning provision, use that route. When a timely accountable answer is required, obtain the appropriate service. Use CB.1 if the proposed mutual activity has no credible benefit or cannot obtain its essential contribution.

### CB.2:2 - Problem

Potential participants often wait for one another. A question is too broad for anyone to answer, a capable helper does not know it exists, or a newcomer sees activity but cannot tell how to join. Public invitations alone may leave these conditions unchanged. Conversely, an organiser can keep every conversation moving so effectively that the group appears self-sustaining while depending on unrecognised work.

How can the organiser obtain a useful first exchange, learn what makes it possible, and support continuation without manufacturing demand or promising unavailable help?

### CB.2:3 - Forces

| Force | Decision it changes |
| --- | --- |
| A small feasible start and relevant variety | A bounded group permits careful matching; selecting only familiar enthusiasts can hide missing perspectives and conditions. |
| Mutuality and unequal contributions | Participants can benefit in different ways and at different times; immediate repayment requirements can exclude useful participation. |
| Organising support and independent initiative | Early preparation can enable a relationship, while invisible constant prompting can conceal dependence. |
| Quality of help and willingness to help | A willing participant may lack the relevant competence; an expert may have no available time or permission. |
| Reach and usable attention | More invitees increase possible connections, but also unanswered requests, preparation and moderation burden. |

### CB.2:4 - Solution

Choose one meaningful exchange, obtain the necessary contributions deliberately, make participation understandable, and close the return to the people involved. Repeat or adapt the arrangement on the basis of what participants could obtain and what support it consumed.

**Working reminder:** choose a useful exchange; prepare both sides; connect them within real limits; return the result; keep or change the support.

#### CB.2:4.1 - Choose an exchange that can finish

Take the purpose from the current proposal rather than inventing an entertaining activity to fill a calendar. Name a small useful result and its recipient. A repair case might end with a justified diagnostic next step. A writers' group might end with an author understanding one reader's difficulty. A neighbourhood meeting might end with two people agreeing a feasible shared action.

Bound the work so that the recipient can supply relevant material and a contributor can respond within the proposed time. A request to “share your expertise” gives neither party enough direction. A request to inspect one permitted observation and explain which of two next steps it supports can.

State what would count as a usable result and what must remain outside the exchange. Use the target practice's criteria for a technical answer. A community organiser can arrange access to a qualified judgement without being qualified to make it. For a social activity, a wanted opportunity to speak and be heard may be the result; do not impose a technical deliverable on that purpose.

If the original request needs a missing domain method, unavailable data or a decision that the participants cannot make, obtain that contribution or revise the activity. A well-run meeting cannot supply it by itself.

#### CB.2:4.2 - Find the contributions needed on each side

Start from the result and work backward to the people, material and conditions that could produce it. For a help request, this normally means a person with a real question and someone with relevant competence, experience or access. For a joint activity, identify the different contributions that make it worthwhile.

Approach potential participants with a specific reason their contribution could matter. Explain the purpose, requested preparation, likely effort, available support and limits. Ask whether they want to participate under those conditions. Being well known, holding a title or appearing on a member list does not establish willingness or suitability for this exchange.

Obtain enough information to make a defensible match. The contributor may need to know the kind of case, the question, the material available and the recipient's starting knowledge. The recipient may need to know the contributor's experience and the limits of the proposed help. Use only information relevant to the match and permissible to share.

Include a consequential contrast when it changes the work: another practice setting, a novice's difficulty, a different schedule, or a perspective the first group lacks. This does not require inviting every stakeholder to the first exchange. It requires not claiming that the first convenient group represents everyone.

Keep the exchange voluntary in its actual terms. A person may ask without yet being able to answer other people's questions. Another may contribute by describing a failure, finding a source, listening carefully or testing an explanation. State the contribution needed for this activity; do not turn every invitation into an obligation to become a leader.

#### CB.2:4.3 - Prepare the request, material and participation conditions

Help the recipient formulate a request that a suitable contributor can act on. For a case discussion, include the situation, what has already been tried, the uncertainty or choice, the permitted material and the requested kind of response. Distinguish an observation from its interpretation. Remove detail that burdens the exchange without changing the question, while preserving conditions that change the answer.

Confirm that the contributors have the necessary material and understand the requested result. If they need preparation, include it in their time commitment. Offer a smaller or different contribution when the whole request is too demanding. An explicit refusal or unavailable contribution is useful information about the arrangement.

Choose the simplest medium that supports the work:

- use asynchronous exchange when participants can supply interpretable material and respond at different times;
- use a bounded live conversation when rapid clarification or joint attention to the same material matters;
- use a private or smaller setting when the permitted material or participants' conditions make a broad space unsuitable.

A written case can prepare a live discussion, and a short conversation can produce a written result. Make the connection between the two explicit.

Agree how people ask for clarification, decline a question, signal uncertainty and obtain help with the interaction. Explain what may be shared beyond the exchange and who decides that. These minimal conditions let the first activity proceed; a wider or continuing arrangement may need more developed entry, norms and governance.

#### CB.2:4.4 - Facilitate the exchange where support is actually needed

At the start, restate the purpose, result and limits. Let the recipient establish the question before contributors begin solving a different one. Make the relevant material available to everyone whose contribution needs it.

Use prompting to repair a specific obstacle. Invite a qualified person to address an unanswered question, ask a contributor to explain the observation behind a conclusion, or help the recipient distinguish two incompatible suggestions. Give enough time for a response and make declining permissible. Repeated unsolicited reminders are not a substitute for an agreed contribution.

When participants disagree, identify the condition or evidence that changes the proposed answer. They may be considering different configurations, using different criteria or lacking a decisive observation. Preserve a warranted unresolved result and the next source or observation needed. Do not force agreement to make the session look successful.

Protect the distinction between interaction and professional action. A repairer receiving advice still needs to judge its applicability and satisfy the conditions governing the actual repair. An author owns changes to a draft. A neighbourhood volunteer can propose an action without acquiring authority over other residents.

Make the organiser's contribution visible in the account of the activity: finding people, preparing the question, clarifying terms, arranging access or following up. Identify which contribution would disappear if that support ended.

#### CB.2:4.5 - Close the return to the recipient and contributors

Ask the recipient what the exchange enables them to do next. Where practical application is possible, obtain a later response about what was attempted, what happened and what remains uncertain. If application has not occurred, record that limit rather than treating silence as failure or treating a promising answer as a completed result.

Return a useful response to the contributor. It may be a short account of how the advice changed the next step, a clarification of what remained difficult, or an explanation that the proposed action could not be tried because a condition was absent. This closes an information gap and can improve the next contribution. It is not a debt requiring the recipient to praise the helper or perform an equal favour.

For a social or shared-interest activity, ask about the experience and whether participants want a next contact. Keep that judgement with them. The organiser cannot infer a valued relationship solely from attendance.

Retain a reusable explanation or case only when its use is appropriate and the necessary permission exists. Preserve the conditions that made it valid. A participant's consent to a small conversation does not automatically supply consent to publishing an attributed account. If no reusable record is needed, a completed private exchange can remain private.

#### CB.2:4.6 - Support the next exchange without hiding its cost

Use what happened to decide what should continue. Did the recipient obtain a useful result? Was the contributor's effort acceptable? Which part needed organising help? What would make a similar exchange easier or more reliable?

The next step may be another deliberately arranged exchange, a modest adjustment to the request, a reusable reference, a change of timing, or a different route under CB.1. Invite continuation on the strength of a concrete useful experience. A member can introduce another suitable person when that person also receives enough information to choose.

Watch for initiative that is compatible with the purpose: a participant brings a new relevant question, offers help under stated limits or arranges contact without the organiser supplying every step. Treat such observations as local evidence of the arrangement's current operation. They do not establish a universal growth stage or the long-term independence of the group.

Reduce or redistribute a particular organising contribution only when the needed work can still be done. If questions are now understandable but contributor matching still needs help, retain matching and stop rewriting every question. If recurring support has no willing, capable provider, reduce the activity, obtain resources or change the promise. Formal responsibilities and broader decision rights require their own agreement.

Do not use group size as the sole continuation test. A small group can deliver the desired contribution. A larger one can become less usable if the relevant questions no longer receive attention.

#### CB.2:4.7 - Recognise returns and assurance limits

| What the exchange establishes | Next useful move |
| --- | --- |
| Useful result, acceptable burden, and willing continuation | Keep the working arrangement and change only a demonstrated obstacle. |
| A relevant question, but no willing capable contributor | Revise the request or terms, obtain another supplier, or return the offer to CB.1. |
| A helpful discussion, but no evidence of application yet | Preserve the useful discussion result and the unanswered application question. |
| Conflicting advice with no available deciding basis | Return the technical or evidential question to a qualified domain contributor; keep the disagreement intelligible. |
| Results depend on organising work that exceeds available support | Reduce scope, redistribute accepted contributions, fund the required work or stop the unsupported promise. |
| A resource or individual service would answer the recurring need more directly | Use that route and retain only the mutual activity that still adds value. |

For stronger claims, obtain the evidence that claim requires. A useful answer is not proof of learning; a few useful exchanges are not a causal estimate of programme benefit; a successful small activity does not establish available capacity at a larger scale.

### CB.2:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### CB.2:5.1 - One repair question receives a usable return

Continue CB.1's constructed repairer case. The organiser finds one owner of a shareable diagnostic question and two repairers with relevant experience who agree to help. The case involves a known professional method; its permitted reference material supplies the technical criteria. These starting facts are stipulated for the example.

The owner prepares the observations, the actions already taken and one unresolved choice. Customer identifiers and material without sharing permission are excluded. The two contributors see the question in advance and confirm that the requested help fits their competence and available time.

The proposed live exchange lasts twenty minutes: four minutes to establish the question, five for each contributor's interpretation, and six for comparison and the next step. These are this case's planning allowances, not a required meeting format. Preparation and closure are included separately:

| Participant | Preparation | Exchange | Return or closure | Total |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Case owner | 8 min | 20 min | 7 min | 35 min |
| Contributor A | 10 min | 20 min | 5 min | 35 min |
| Contributor B | 10 min | 20 min | 5 min | 35 min |
| Organiser | 10 min | 20 min | 10 min | 40 min |

The planned activity consumes 145 person-minutes. If a contributor has only twenty minutes including preparation, this arrangement does not fit that person's availability. The organiser can obtain a narrower written contribution, change the window or choose a different capable person; the missing preparation is not made free by calling the event short.

During the exchange, the two proposed next steps turn out to assume different equipment configurations. The group identifies the missing configuration fact and an authorised way for the owner to obtain it. The result is a justified information-gathering next step, not a diagnosis reached by consensus.

The owner later reports that the new information made one of the proposed diagnostic routes inapplicable and allowed the known procedure to continue. Both helpers receive that response. A short conditional note can be retained with the owner's agreement. It says which configuration changes the choice, so the account does not become an unconditional repair recipe.

For the next exchange, the owner offers to explain this configuration distinction to someone facing a similar question. That is a real contribution to consider. It does not establish that the owner can now judge every unusual fault or lead the whole group.

#### CB.2:5.2 - The same purpose requires asynchronous participation

In a second constructed use, the repairers' working hours do not overlap. The organiser keeps the purpose and permitted material but changes the interaction. The owner posts the bounded question and indicates when a response would still be useful. Each contributor accepts a feasible response window; one answers and the other checks the conditional interpretation later.

The owner returns a concise account of the chosen next step. If the decision cannot wait for those windows, the professional service route replaces this exchange for that case.
